## Issue #592: [Bug]: MiniMax system role rejection persists after PR #586

- **State:** CLOSED
- **Author:** dmitriikeler (Dmitri Keler)
- **Labels:** none
- **Comments:** none

**Body:**

MiniMax API rejects messages with `role: system` with error:
```
invalid params, chat content has invalid message role: system (2013)
```

This occurs on both direct Telegram chat and cron job execution. PR #586 was merged to fix this ("restore MiniMax chat/completions request shaping so system prompts stay in `messages[]` instead of being moved into a top-level `system` field") but the error persists on `20260408.01`.

Note: the PR's live MiniMax smoke test checkbox was unchecked -- the fix was verified with mocks only, not against the actual MiniMax API.

**Expected behavior:** MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed should accept the request. System prompts should either stay as `role: system` in messages (if MiniMax supports it) or be converted to a format MiniMax accepts (e.g. prepended to the first user message, or sent as a top-level parameter if their API supports one).

**Steps to reproduce:**
1. Configure a custom OpenAI-compatible provider for MiniMax with `MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed`
2. Send a message via Telegram or trigger a cron job
3. Error: `invalid params, chat content has invalid message role: system (2013)`

**Environment:** moltis 20260408.01, Fly.io container (Debian slim), MiniMax Plus-Highspeed plan. The reporter notes this is a regression.

---

## Issue #582: [Bug]: MiniMax 2.7 `spawn_agent` rejects `null` for optional array parameters (`allow_tools`, `deny_tools`)

- **State:** CLOSED
- **Author:** stratus-ss (Steve Ovens)
- **Labels:** `bug`
- **Comments:** none

**Body:**

When MiniMax calls `spawn_agent` and passes `null` for the optional `allow_tools` and `deny_tools` parameters, Moltis rejects the call with a misleading error: `missing required parameter: task` -- even though `task` is present and valid. The actual failure is deserialization of `null` for array-typed fields. When the same call uses `[]` instead of `null`, it succeeds.

MiniMax gets stuck in a loop and sometimes reaches the agent loop max. Other models like Nemotron 3 Super handle this without issue.

**Expected behavior:** `null` should be treated as equivalent to an empty array `[]` (or the parameter's default) for optional array parameters. Many LLMs include all schema-defined parameters in their tool calls and use `null` for "not specified."

**Steps to reproduce:**
1. Use a model that includes all tool parameters in calls (e.g., MiniMax M2.7)
2. Have the model call `spawn_agent` -- it produces `"allow_tools": null, "deny_tools": null` etc.
3. Moltis returns: `parameter 'allow_tools' must be an array`
4. On retry with `[]` instead of `null`, the call succeeds

**Environment:** moltis 20260406.05, Arch Linux, pre-built binary. The reporter attached several config files (SKILL.md, HOOK.md, handler scripts, AGENTS.md).

---

## Issue #578: [Bug] [minimax] Channel sessions have empty system prompt -- no identity, soul, skills, workspace files, or boot instructions injected

- **State:** CLOSED
- **Author:** dmitriikeler (Dmitri Keler)
- **Labels:** none
- **Comments:** 5 (4 from dmitriikeler, 1 from penso)

**Body:**

Channel sessions (Telegram, Discord) receive only the bare model default system prompt. No workspace files are injected at all. The agent identifies as "MiniMax-M2.7" instead of the configured name, has no operating rules, can't use skills, and doesn't know the user.

The reporter provided a detailed table showing that IDENTITY.md, SOUL.md, USER.md, AGENTS.md, TOOLS.md, BOOT.md, and skills all exist on disk with correct permissions but none are injected into the system prompt for channel sessions.

**Environment:** MOLTIS v20260405.06, Linux x86_64, Fly.io, MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed provider.

**Comment thread progression (root cause investigation by dmitriikeler):**

1. **Update 1:** Web UI also partially affected -- IDENTITY.md frontmatter (name/emoji) IS injected in web UI but NOT in channels. SOUL.md body, AGENTS.md, TOOLS.md, skills are NOT injected anywhere.

2. **Update 2:** Agent ID mismatch ruled out -- files exist at both `data_dir()/` and `data_dir()/agents/main/`. Streaming mode ruled out. All environmental causes eliminated. Concluded it appeared to be a code bug in prompt assembly.

3. **Update 3 -- ROOT CAUSE FOUND:** Using `chat.fullContext` GraphQL query, confirmed the system prompt IS fully assembled (34,098 chars). The issue is **MiniMax-specific**: Moltis moves system messages to a top-level `"system"` JSON field (workaround for issue #508 -- MiniMax error 2013 on `role: "system"` in messages array). MiniMax's `/v1/chat/completions` endpoint silently ignores this non-standard field, so the model receives zero system prompt.

4. **Update 4 -- RESOLVED:** Confirmed by switching to Anthropic provider, which correctly receives the full system prompt. The bug is specifically that MiniMax system prompt injection via the top-level `"system"` field doesn't work. Suggested fixes: prepend as first user message, check if MiniMax now supports `role: "system"`, or add per-provider config for system prompt injection strategy.

5. **penso (maintainer):** "Thanks for the detailed issue and your search on this. Looking at it."

---

**Summary of the three issues:** All three are related to MiniMax provider compatibility. #578 identified that MiniMax silently ignores the top-level `system` field workaround. #592 reports that PR #586 (which attempted to fix the system prompt issue) didn't actually resolve the `role: system` rejection against the live MiniMax API. #582 is about MiniMax sending `null` instead of `[]` for optional array tool parameters, causing `spawn_agent` deserialization failures.

## Comprehensive MiniMax Provider Implementation Analysis

### 1. WHERE MINIMAX IS IMPLEMENTED

- `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/nervous-ironclad/crates/config/src/validate.rs` - Provider name validation

### 2. MODEL LISTING AND CONFIGURATION

**Model Catalog (7 models):**

```rust
const MINIMAX_MODELS: &[(&str, &str)] = &[
    ("MiniMax-M2.7", "MiniMax M2.7"),
    ("MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed", "MiniMax M2.7 Highspeed"),
    ("MiniMax-M2.5", "MiniMax M2.5"),

    ("MiniMax-M2.1", "MiniMax M2.1"),
    ("MiniMax-M2.1-highspeed", "MiniMax M2.1 Highspeed"),
    ("MiniMax-M2", "MiniMax M2"),
];
```

**Provider Registration (in `OPENAI_COMPAT_PROVIDERS`):**

```rust
OpenAiCompatDef {
    config_name: "minimax",
    env_key: "MINIMAX_API_KEY",
    env_base_url_key: "MINIMAX_BASE_URL",
    default_base_url: "https://api.minimax.io/v1",
    models: MINIMAX_MODELS,
    supports_model_discovery: false,  // MiniMax does not expose /models endpoint
    requires_api_key: true,
    local_only: false,
}
```

**Context Window:**
- MiniMax M2/M2.1/M2.5/M2.7: **204,800 tokens** (defined in `get_context_window()`)

---

### 3. STREAMING IMPLEMENTATION

MiniMax uses **OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions API** with SSE streaming:

**Streaming Flow (in `openai.rs`):**

1. `stream_with_tools()` → Converts typed `ChatMessage` to OpenAI format via `serialize_messages_for_request()`
2. Applies system prompt extraction (see section 4)
3. Posts to `{base_url}/chat/completions` with `stream=true`
4. **SSE Processing:** Uses `process_openai_sse_line()` from `openai_compat.rs` to parse `data: {...}` frames
5. **State Machine:** `StreamingToolState` tracks incomplete tool calls across frames
6. **Finalization:** `finalize_stream()` emits final `StreamEvent::Done(usage)` with token counts

**Key SSE Parsing Logic:**
- Parses `choices[0].delta.content` for text deltas
- Handles `tool_calls` array with incremental updates via `json_merge()`
- Supports `finish_reason: "stop"` or `"function_calls"` or streaming completion
- Falls back to usage data if trailing `[DONE]` is missing (MiniMax may close connection early)

**Example Streaming Test:**
```rust
async fn minimax_stream_request_extracts_system_role_messages()
// Tests that system messages are moved to top-level "system" field
```

---

### 4. SYSTEM PROMPT HANDLING (CRITICAL)

MiniMax has a **unique constraint**: it rejects `role: "system"` entries in the messages array and requires system content in a top-level `"system"` field instead.

**Detection Logic (in `openai.rs`):**
```rust
fn requires_top_level_system_prompt(&self) -> bool {
    self.model.starts_with("MiniMax-")
        || self.provider_name.eq_ignore_ascii_case("minimax")
        || self.base_url.to_ascii_lowercase().contains("minimax")
}
```

**Transformation (in `apply_top_level_system_prompt()`):**
1. Scans request body's `messages` array
2. Extracts all `role: "system"` entries
3. Joins their text content with `\n\n`
4. Places result in top-level `body["system"]`
5. Removes system messages from messages array

**Request Structure:**
```json
{
  "model": "MiniMax-M2.7",
  "system": "system prompt text here",
  "messages": [
    {"role": "user", "content": "..."}
  ]
}
```

**Regression Tests:**
- `minimax_body_extracts_system_messages_to_top_level()` - unit test
- `minimax_complete_request_extracts_system_role_messages_regression_578()` - non-streaming completion
- `minimax_stream_request_extracts_system_role_messages()` - streaming
- `minimax_stream_extracts_multiple_system_messages_regression_578()` - multiple system messages

---

### 5. TOOL CALL HANDLING

MiniMax uses **OpenAI-compatible function calling** format:

**Request Side:**
1. Converts tool schemas via `to_openai_tools()` (Chat Completions format)
2. Serializes with `tool_choice: "auto"` by default
3. Applies system prompt extraction
4. Tool call IDs may be remapped if > 64 chars (via `assign_openai_tool_call_id()`)

**Response Side:**
1. Parses `choices[0].message.tool_calls[]` array (non-streaming)
2. For streaming: incrementally accumulates `tool_calls` deltas via `json_merge()`
3. Each tool call has: `{"id": "call_...", "function": {"name": "...", "arguments": "..."}}`
4. Tool result follows as `ChatMessage::Tool { tool_call_id, content }`

**Note on Tool Params:**
- Recent fix (PR referenced in session memo): Handles `null` values for optional `allow_tools`/`deny_tools` arrays
- Helper: `string_array_param()` in `crates/tools/src/params.rs` treats missing/null as absent

### 7. PROVIDER TEST INFRASTRUCTURE

**Mock Server Pattern (in `openai.rs` tests):**

```rust
struct CapturedRequest {
    body: Option<serde_json::Value>,
}

async fn start_sse_mock(sse_payload: String) 
    -> (String, Arc<Mutex<Vec<CapturedRequest>>>)
// Creates an Axum router at random localhost port
// Returns (base_url, captured_requests)
// POST /chat/completions returns SSE with given payload
// Captures request body JSON for assertion

async fn start_completion_mock(json_payload: String)
    -> (String, Arc<Mutex<Vec<CapturedRequest>>>)
// Same pattern for non-streaming JSON responses
```

**Contract Test Framework (in `contract.rs`):**

Reusable behavior tests for any `LlmProvider`:
- `non_stream_returns_complete_response()` - completion has text and usage
- `stream_emits_done_signal()` - stream must emit final `Done` event
- `stream_surfaces_reasoning_separately()` - reasoning deltas precede visible deltas
- `error_classification_maps_429_to_retryable()`
- `error_classification_maps_401_to_fatal()`

Can be run against any provider via:
```rust
crate::contract::stream_surfaces_reasoning_separately(&provider).await
```

**Anthropic Tests Pattern (comparable):**

The Anthropic provider (`anthropic.rs`) uses similar patterns:
- Mock server helpers (`start_probe_mock()`, `start_models_mock()`)
- Captures request bodies to validate shape
- 20+ tests covering serialization, probes, model discovery, thinking budget

---

### 8. INTEGRATION TEST PATTERNS & INFRASTRUCTURE

**No Real API Key Integration Tests in Providers Crate:**

The `crates/providers/src/` has **only unit tests with mocked HTTP servers**. No tests use real API keys or live MiniMax API.

**Plan for Integration Testing:**

Per `/plans/2026-03-01-plan-contract-test-harness-for-integrations.md`:
- **Status:** Channel contracts done; Provider contracts not started
- **Scope:** Provider contract suite should validate:
  1. Non-stream and stream response parity
  2. Tool-call handling behavior
  3. Error classification mapping (retryable vs fatal)
  4. Rate-limit handling contracts

**E2E Testing Approach:**

Per `/docs/src/e2e-testing.md`:
- Browser-level tests in `crates/web/ui/e2e/specs/` using Playwright
- Runs real gateway process in isolated test directories
- Web UI tests, not provider integration tests
- Example: `onboarding.spec.js` tests provider selection UI
- Run via: `just ui-e2e` or `just ui-e2e-headed`

**Existing Integration Tests:**
- `crates/graphql/tests/integration.rs` - GraphQL-level tests
- `crates/webhooks/tests/ingress_integration.rs` - Webhook integration
- `crates/httpd/tests/` - Gateway HTTP middleware and auth tests

**Pattern for Adding Real Provider Tests:**

Would need:
1. Separate crate or test module: `crates/providers/tests/integration.rs`
2. Guard with `#[cfg_attr(not(feature = "integration-tests"), ignore)]`
3. Require env var: `MINIMAX_API_KEY` (from CI secrets)
4. Use contract functions from `contract.rs` against real provider
5. CI enforcement: Add `./scripts/local-validate.sh` check for integration test gate

---

### 9. KEY DESIGN PATTERNS

**1. OpenAI-Compatible Architecture:**
- Single `OpenAiProvider` struct handles 10+ OpenAI-compatible providers
- Provider-specific behavior via flag methods (e.g., `requires_top_level_system_prompt()`)
- Router-driven registration via `OPENAI_COMPAT_PROVIDERS` array

**2. Message Transformation Pipeline:**
```
ChatMessage[] 
  → serialize_messages_for_request() 
  → apply_top_level_system_prompt() [MiniMax-specific]
  → apply_reasoning_effort_chat() [o1/o3-specific]
  → apply_openrouter_cache_control() [OpenRouter-specific]
```

**3. Streaming State Machine:**
- `StreamingToolState` accumulates delta fragments
- Tool calls built incrementally via `json_merge()`
- Final `finalize_stream()` emits Done with usage

**4. Context Window Resolution:**
```rust
fn get_context_window(model_id: &str) -> usize {
    if model_id.starts_with("MiniMax-") { 204_800 }
    else if model_id.starts_with("gpt-") { /* ... */ }
    else { 200_000 }  // default
}
```

**5. Probe Pattern:**
- Every provider has `probe()` to validate API connectivity
- Sends minimal request (1 token output) to avoid cost
- MiniMax uses `max_tokens: 1` (not `max_completion_tokens`)
- Includes test coverage

---

### 10. CRITICAL IMPLEMENTATION DETAILS

**Token Usage Parsing:**
```rust
// MiniMax uses Anthropic-style token names:
// "input_tokens", "output_tokens" (not "prompt_tokens", "completion_tokens")
// Handled in: parse_openai_compat_usage() and parse_openai_compat_usage_from_payload()
```

**API Endpoint:**
- Default: `https://api.minimax.io/v1/chat/completions`
- Reference: https://platform.minimax.io/docs/api-reference/text-anthropic-api

**Known Quirks:**
1. Does NOT expose `/models` endpoint (returns 404) → `supports_model_discovery: false`
2. Rejects system messages in array → requires top-level `system` field
3. May close SSE connection without `[DONE]` frame → fallback to usage-only finalization
4. Tool calls with `null` optional params need lenient parsing → `string_array_param()` helper

---

### SUMMARY

MiniMax is implemented as an **OpenAI-compatible provider** in the unified `OpenAiProvider` struct. It reuses:
- Chat Completions API format for requests
- SSE streaming with delta accumulation
- Function-calling (tool) format
- Token counting via usage metadata

Its **key distinction** is the **top-level system prompt field**, which is detected by model name prefix, provider name, or base URL and applied at request serialization time. This is covered by **5 dedicated regression tests** plus **2 registration tests**.

Tests use **mocked Axum HTTP servers** that capture request bodies, validating request shape without touching real APIs. Integration test infrastructure exists as a plan (`contract.rs` framework) but is not yet implemented for providers.

    49→/// Initialize the shared provider HTTP client with optional upstream proxy.
    50→///
    51→/// Call once at gateway startup; subsequent calls are no-ops.
    52→pub fn init_shared_http_client(proxy_url: Option<&str>) {
    53→    let _ = SHARED_CLIENT.set(moltis_common::http_client::build_http_client(proxy_url));
    54→}
    55→
    56→/// Shared HTTP client for LLM providers.
    57→///
    58→/// All providers that don't need custom redirect/proxy settings should
    59→/// reuse this client to share connection pools, DNS cache, and TLS sessions.
    60→///
    61→/// Falls back to a plain client if [`init_shared_http_client`] was never
    62→/// called (e.g. in tests).
    63→pub fn shared_http_client() -> &'static reqwest::Client {
    64→    SHARED_CLIENT.get_or_init(reqwest::Client::new)
    65→}

     1→//! Contract tests for the [`LlmProvider`] trait.
     2→//!
     3→//! These functions validate that any `LlmProvider` implementation satisfies
     4→//! the completion and streaming semantics required by the chat runtime.
     5→//! Run against `MockLlmProvider` in provider tests.
     6→
     7→#![allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
     8→
     9→use std::pin::Pin;
    10→
    11→use {
    12→    async_trait::async_trait,
    13→    moltis_agents::model::{ChatMessage, CompletionResponse, LlmProvider, StreamEvent, Usage},
    14→    tokio_stream::{Stream, StreamExt},
    15→};
    16→
    17→// ── Mock provider ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    18→
    19→/// A mock LLM provider that returns canned responses for contract testing.
    20→pub struct MockLlmProvider {
    21→    /// If set, `complete()` returns this error.
    22→    pub complete_error: Option<MockError>,
    23→}
    24→
    25→/// Simulated error types for the mock provider.
    26→pub enum MockError {
    27→    /// Simulates a 429 rate-limit error (retryable).
    28→    RateLimit,
    29→    /// Simulates a 401 auth error (fatal).
    30→    AuthFailed,
    31→}
    32→
    33→impl MockLlmProvider {
    34→    pub fn ok() -> Self {
    35→        Self {
    36→            complete_error: None,
    37→        }
    38→    }
    39→
    40→    pub fn with_error(error: MockError) -> Self {
    41→        Self {
    42→            complete_error: Some(error),
    43→        }
    44→    }
    45→}
    46→
    47→#[async_trait]
    48→impl LlmProvider for MockLlmProvider {
    49→    fn name(&self) -> &str {
    50→        "mock"
    51→    }
    52→
    53→    fn id(&self) -> &str {
    54→        "mock-model"
    55→    }
    56→
    57→    async fn complete(
    58→        &self,
    59→        _messages: &[ChatMessage],
    60→        _tools: &[serde_json::Value],
    61→    ) -> anyhow::Result<CompletionResponse> {
    62→        match &self.complete_error {
    63→            Some(MockError::RateLimit) => {
    64→                anyhow::bail!("429 Too Many Requests: rate limited")
    65→            },
    66→            Some(MockError::AuthFailed) => {
    67→                anyhow::bail!("401 Unauthorized: invalid API key")
    68→            },
    69→            None => Ok(CompletionResponse {
    70→                text: Some("Hello from mock provider".into()),
    71→                tool_calls: vec![],
    72→                usage: Usage {
    73→                    input_tokens: 10,
    74→                    output_tokens: 5,
    75→                    cache_read_tokens: 0,
    76→                    cache_write_tokens: 0,
    77→                },
    78→            }),
    79→        }
    80→    }
    81→
    82→    fn stream(
    83→        &self,
    84→        _messages: Vec<ChatMessage>,
    85→    ) -> Pin<Box<dyn Stream<Item = StreamEvent> + Send + '_>> {
    86→        let events = vec![
    87→            StreamEvent::Delta("Hello ".into()),
    88→            StreamEvent::Delta("world".into()),
    89→            StreamEvent::Done(Usage {
    90→                input_tokens: 10,
    91→                output_tokens: 5,
    92→                cache_read_tokens: 0,
    93→                cache_write_tokens: 0,
    94→            }),
    95→        ];
    96→        Box::pin(tokio_stream::iter(events))
    97→    }
    98→}
    99→

   100→// ── Contract tests ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   101→
   102→/// A non-streaming completion must return a response with text and usage data.
   103→pub async fn non_stream_returns_complete_response(
   104→    provider: &dyn LlmProvider,
   105→) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
   106→    let messages = vec![ChatMessage::user("Say hello.")];
   107→    let response = provider.complete(&messages, &[]).await?;
   108→
   109→    assert!(
   110→        response.text.is_some(),
   111→        "completion response must contain text"
   112→    );
   113→    assert!(
   114→        response.usage.input_tokens > 0 || response.usage.output_tokens > 0,
   115→        "completion response must include usage data"
   116→    );
   117→    Ok(())
   118→}
   119→
   120→/// A streaming completion must eventually emit a Done event.
   121→pub async fn stream_emits_done_signal(provider: &dyn LlmProvider) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
   122→    let messages = vec![ChatMessage::user("Say hello.")];
   123→    let mut stream = provider.stream(messages);
   124→
   125→    let mut saw_done = false;
   126→    while let Some(event) = stream.next().await {
   127→        if matches!(event, StreamEvent::Done(_)) {
   128→            saw_done = true;
   129→            break;
   130→        }
   131→    }
   132→
   133→    assert!(saw_done, "stream must emit a Done event");
   134→    Ok(())
   135→}
   136→
   137→/// A streaming completion may surface reasoning separately from visible text.
   138→///
   139→/// Providers that support explicit reasoning channels should emit at least one
   140→/// `ReasoningDelta`, then at least one visible `Delta`, and still finish with
   141→/// `Done`.
   142→pub async fn stream_surfaces_reasoning_separately(
   143→    provider: &dyn LlmProvider,
   144→) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
   145→    let messages = vec![ChatMessage::user("Think, then answer.")];
   146→    let mut stream = provider.stream(messages);
   147→
   148→    let mut first_reasoning_index: Option<usize> = None;
   149→    let mut first_visible_index: Option<usize> = None;
   150→    let mut saw_done = false;
   151→    let mut event_index = 0usize;
   152→
   153→    while let Some(event) = stream.next().await {
   154→        match event {
   155→            StreamEvent::ReasoningDelta(_) => {
   156→                first_reasoning_index.get_or_insert(event_index);
   157→            },
   158→            StreamEvent::Delta(_) => {
   159→                first_visible_index.get_or_insert(event_index);
   160→            },
   161→            StreamEvent::Done(_) => {
   162→                saw_done = true;
   163→                break;
   164→            },
   165→            _ => {},
   166→        }
   167→        event_index += 1;
   168→    }
   169→
   170→    let first_reasoning_index = first_reasoning_index
   171→        .ok_or_else(|| anyhow::anyhow!("stream should emit at least one ReasoningDelta"))?;
   172→    let first_visible_index = first_visible_index
   173→        .ok_or_else(|| anyhow::anyhow!("stream should emit at least one visible Delta"))?;
   174→    assert!(
   175→        first_reasoning_index < first_visible_index,
   176→        "reasoning should arrive before visible text"
   177→    );
   178→    assert!(saw_done, "stream must emit a Done event");
   179→    Ok(())
   180→}
   181→
   182→/// A 429 rate-limit error message must contain "429" (retryable indicator).
   183→pub async fn error_classification_maps_429_to_retryable(provider: &dyn LlmProvider) {
   184→    let messages = vec![ChatMessage::user("test")];
   185→    let result = provider.complete(&messages, &[]).await;

   193→

   710→            || self.model.starts_with("kimi-")
   711→    }
   712→
   713→    /// Some providers (e.g. MiniMax) reject `role: "system"` in the messages
   714→    /// array and require system content in a top-level `"system"` field instead.
   715→    /// Add new providers here when they have the same constraint.
   716→    fn requires_top_level_system_prompt(&self) -> bool {
   717→        self.model.starts_with("MiniMax-")
   718→            || self.provider_name.eq_ignore_ascii_case("minimax")
   719→            || self.base_url.to_ascii_lowercase().contains("minimax")
   720→    }
   721→
   722→    /// For providers that reject `role: "system"` in the messages array,
   723→    /// extract all system messages from `body["messages"]`, join their
   724→    /// content with `\n\n`, and place the result in `body["system"]`.
   725→    ///
   726→    /// Must be called on the request body **after** it is fully assembled.
   727→    /// This is intentionally a single body-level mutation so call sites
   728→    /// cannot forget to apply it (previously reverted by accident in #586).
   729→    fn apply_top_level_system_prompt(&self, body: &mut serde_json::Value) {
   730→        if !self.requires_top_level_system_prompt() {
   731→            return;
   732→        }
   733→        let Some(messages) = body
   734→            .get_mut("messages")
   735→            .and_then(serde_json::Value::as_array_mut)
   736→        else {
   737→            return;
   738→        };
   739→        let mut system_parts = Vec::new();
   740→        messages.retain(|msg| {
   741→            if msg.get("role").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str) == Some("system") {
   742→                if let Some(content) = msg.get("content").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)
   743→                    && !content.is_empty()
   744→                {
   745→                    system_parts.push(content.to_string());
   746→                } else if msg.get("content").is_some() {
   747→                    warn!("MiniMax system message has non-string content; it will be dropped");
   748→                }
   749→                return false;
   750→            }
   751→            true
   752→        });
   753→        if !system_parts.is_empty() {
   754→            body["system"] = serde_json::Value::String(system_parts.join("\n\n"));
   755→        }
   756→    }
   757→
   758→    fn serialize_messages_for_request(&self, messages: &[ChatMessage]) -> Vec<serde_json::Value> {
   759→        let needs_reasoning_content = self.requires_reasoning_content_on_tool_messages();
   760→        let mut remapped_tool_call_ids = HashMap::new();
   761→        let mut used_tool_call_ids = HashSet::new();
   762→        let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(messages.len());
   763→
   764→        for message in messages {
   765→            let mut value = message.to_openai_value();
   766→
   767→            if let Some(tool_calls) = value
   768→                .get_mut("tool_calls")
   769→                .and_then(serde_json::Value::as_array_mut)
   770→            {
   771→                for tool_call in tool_calls {
   772→                    let Some(tool_call_id) =
   773→                        tool_call.get("id").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)
   774→                    else {
   775→                        continue;
   776→                    };
   777→                    let mapped_id = assign_openai_tool_call_id(
   778→                        tool_call_id,
   779→                        &mut remapped_tool_call_ids,
   780→                        &mut used_tool_call_ids,
   781→                    );
   782→                    tool_call["id"] = serde_json::Value::String(mapped_id);
   783→                }
   784→            } else if value.get("role").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str) == Some("tool")
   785→                && let Some(tool_call_id) = value
   786→                    .get("tool_call_id")
   787→                    .and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)
   788→            {
   789→                let mapped_id = remapped_tool_call_ids
   790→                    .get(tool_call_id)
   791→                    .cloned()
   792→                    .unwrap_or_else(|| {
   793→                        assign_openai_tool_call_id(
   794→                            tool_call_id,
   795→                            &mut remapped_tool_call_ids,
   796→                            &mut used_tool_call_ids,
   797→                        )
   798→                    });
   799→                value["tool_call_id"] = serde_json::Value::String(mapped_id);
   800→            }
   801→
   802→            if needs_reasoning_content {
   803→                let is_assistant =
   804→                    value.get("role").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str) == Some("assistant");
   805→                let has_tool_calls = value
   806→                    .get("tool_calls")
   807→                    .and_then(serde_json::Value::as_array)
   808→                    .is_some_and(|calls| !calls.is_empty());
   809→

   520→        }
   521→    }
   522→
   523→    /// Apply `reasoning_effort` for the **Responses** API (used by
   524→    /// `stream_with_tools_websocket()`).
   525→    ///
   526→    /// Format: `"reasoning": { "effort": "high" }` (nested object).
   527→    fn apply_reasoning_effort_responses(&self, body: &mut serde_json::Value) {
   528→        if let Some(effort) = self.reasoning_effort_str() {
   529→            body["reasoning"] = serde_json::json!({ "effort": effort });
   530→        }
   531→    }
   532→
   533→    fn apply_probe_output_cap_chat(&self, body: &mut serde_json::Value) {
   534→        let raw = super::raw_model_id(&self.model).to_ascii_lowercase();
   535→        let capability = raw.rsplit('/').next().unwrap_or(raw.as_str());
   536→        let uses_max_completion_tokens = capability.starts_with("gpt-5")
   537→            || capability.starts_with("o1")
   538→            || capability.starts_with("o3")
   539→            || capability.starts_with("o4");
   540→        if uses_max_completion_tokens {
   541→            body["max_completion_tokens"] = serde_json::json!(1);
   542→        } else {
   543→            body["max_tokens"] = serde_json::json!(1);
   544→        }
   545→    }
   546→
   547→    async fn probe_chat_completions(&self) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
   548→        let messages = vec![ChatMessage::user("ping")];
   549→        let mut openai_messages = self.serialize_messages_for_request(&messages);
   550→        self.apply_openrouter_cache_control(&mut openai_messages);
   551→        let mut body = serde_json::json!({
   552→            "model": self.model,
   553→            "messages": openai_messages,
   554→        });
   555→        self.apply_top_level_system_prompt(&mut body);
   556→        // Probes only answer "can this model respond at all?".
   557→        // Keep them cheap instead of mirroring full reasoning budgets.
   558→        self.apply_probe_output_cap_chat(&mut body);
   559→
   560→        debug!(model = %self.model, "openai probe request");
   561→        trace!(body = %serde_json::to_string(&body).unwrap_or_default(), "openai probe request body");
   562→
   563→        let http_resp = self
   564→            .client
   565→            .post(format!("{}/chat/completions", self.base_url))
   566→            .header(
   567→                "Authorization",
   568→                format!("Bearer {}", self.api_key.expose_secret()),
   569→            )

    12→// ── Typed chat messages ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    13→
    14→/// Typed chat message for the LLM provider interface.
    15→///
    16→/// Only contains LLM-relevant fields — metadata like `created_at`, `model`,
    17→/// `provider`, `inputTokens`, `outputTokens` cannot exist here, so they
    18→/// can never leak into provider API requests.
    19→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    20→pub enum ChatMessage {
    21→    System {
    22→        content: String,
    23→    },
    24→    User {
    25→        content: UserContent,
    26→    },
    27→    Assistant {
    28→        content: Option<String>,
    29→        tool_calls: Vec<ToolCall>,
    30→    },
    31→    Tool {
    32→        tool_call_id: String,
    33→        content: String,
    34→    },
    35→}
    36→
    37→/// User message content: plain text or multimodal (text + images).
    38→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    39→pub enum UserContent {
    40→    Text(String),
    41→    Multimodal(Vec<ContentPart>),
    42→}
    43→
    44→impl UserContent {
    45→    /// Create a text-only user content.
    46→    pub fn text(s: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
    47→        Self::Text(s.into())
    48→    }
    49→}
    50→
    51→/// A single part of a multimodal content array.
    52→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    53→pub enum ContentPart {
    54→    Text(String),
    55→    Image { media_type: String, data: String },
    56→}
    57→
    58→impl ChatMessage {
    59→    /// Create a system message.
    60→    pub fn system(content: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
    61→        Self::System {
    62→            content: content.into(),
    63→        }
    64→    }
    65→
    66→    /// Create a user message with plain text.
    67→    pub fn user(content: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
    68→        Self::User {
    69→            content: UserContent::Text(content.into()),
    70→        }
    71→    }
    72→
    73→    /// Create a user message with multimodal content.
    74→    pub fn user_multimodal(parts: Vec<ContentPart>) -> Self {
    75→        Self::User {
    76→            content: UserContent::Multimodal(parts),
    77→        }
    78→    }
    79→
    80→    /// Create an assistant message with text only (no tool calls).
    81→    pub fn assistant(content: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
    82→        Self::Assistant {
    83→            content: Some(content.into()),
    84→            tool_calls: vec![],
    85→        }
    86→    }
    87→
    88→    /// Create an assistant message with tool calls (and optional text).
    89→    pub fn assistant_with_tools(content: Option<String>, tool_calls: Vec<ToolCall>) -> Self {

    92→            tool_calls,
    93→        }

    30→        .ok_or_else(|| Error::message(format!("missing required parameter: {key}")))
    31→}
    32→
    33→/// Extract an optional array of trimmed, non-empty strings.
    34→///
    35→/// Returns an empty vector when the key is absent or explicitly `null`.
    36→pub fn string_array_param(params: &Value, key: &str) -> crate::Result<Vec<String>> {
    37→    let Some(raw) = params.get(key) else {
    38→        return Ok(Vec::new());
    39→    };
    40→    if raw.is_null() {
    41→        return Ok(Vec::new());
    42→    }
    43→
    44→    let arr = raw
    45→        .as_array()
    46→        .ok_or_else(|| Error::message(format!("parameter '{key}' must be an array")))?;
    47→
    48→    let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(arr.len());
    49→    for (idx, item) in arr.iter().enumerate() {
    50→        let name = item
    51→            .as_str()
    52→            .ok_or_else(|| Error::message(format!("parameter '{key}[{idx}]' must be a string")))?;
    53→        let trimmed = name.trim();
    54→        if trimmed.is_empty() {
    55→            return Err(Error::message(format!(
    56→                "parameter '{key}[{idx}]' cannot be empty"
    57→            )));
    58→        }
    59→        out.push(trimmed.to_string());

   354→/// LLM provider trait (Anthropic, OpenAI, Google, etc.).
   355→#[async_trait]

   371→    fn supports_tools(&self) -> bool {
   372→        false
   373→    }
   374→

   378→        200_000

   381→    /// Whether this provider supports vision (image inputs).

   385→        false

   389→    ///
   390→    /// Returns `None` when the provider has no explicit tool mode override

   394→    }
   395→
   396→    /// Stream a completion, yielding delta/done/error events.
   397→    fn stream(
   398→        &self,
   399→        messages: Vec<ChatMessage>,
   400→    ) -> Pin<Box<dyn Stream<Item = StreamEvent> + Send + '_>>;
   401→
   402→    /// Stream a completion with tool support.
   403→    ///
   404→    /// Like `stream()`, but accepts tool schemas and can emit `ToolCallStart`,
   405→    /// `ToolCallArgumentsDelta`, and `ToolCallComplete` events in addition to
   406→    /// text deltas.
   407→    ///
   408→    /// Default implementation falls back to `stream()` (ignoring tools).
   409→    /// Providers with native streaming tool support should override this.
   410→    fn stream_with_tools(
   411→        &self,
   412→        messages: Vec<ChatMessage>,
   413→        _tools: Vec<serde_json::Value>,
   414→    ) -> Pin<Box<dyn Stream<Item = StreamEvent> + Send + '_>> {

   423→        None
   424→    }
   425→
   426→    /// Return a new provider with reasoning effort set, if supported.
   427→    ///
   428→    /// Returns `None` for providers that don't support reasoning effort.
   429→    /// Used by sub-agent spawning to apply per-agent reasoning settings
   430→    /// without mutating the shared registry provider.
   431→    fn with_reasoning_effort(

   435→        None

   437→
   438→    /// Send the cheapest request available that proves the model can answer.
   439→    ///
   440→    /// The default implementation streams a tiny prompt and returns as soon as
   441→    /// the first text delta or terminal event arrives. Providers can override
   442→    /// this to use provider-specific low-cost probe requests.
   443→    async fn probe(&self) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
   444→        let probe = vec![ChatMessage::user("ping")];
   445→        let mut stream = self.stream(probe);
   446→
   447→        let result = tokio::time::timeout(Duration::from_secs(30), async {
   448→            while let Some(event) = stream.next().await {
   449→                match event {
   450→                    StreamEvent::Delta(_) | StreamEvent::Done(_) => return Ok(()),
   451→                    StreamEvent::Error(err) => return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(err)),
   452→                    _ => continue,
   453→                }
   454→            }
   455→            Err(anyhow::anyhow!("stream ended without producing any output"))
   456→        })
   457→        .await;
   458→
   459→        drop(stream);

  "content": "//! Live integration tests for the MiniMax provider.\n//!\n//! These tests hit the real MiniMax API and require `MINIMAX_API_KEY` in the\n//! environment. They are `#[ignore]`d by default so `cargo test` skips them.\n//!\n//! Run with:\n//!   cargo test --test minimax_integration -- --ignored\n//!\n//! Covers regressions for:\n//! - #578: system prompt not injected (silently ignored top-level `system` field)\n//! - #582: `null` for optional array tool parameters (`allow_tools`, `deny_tools`)\n//! - #592: MiniMax rejects `role: system` in messages array\n\nuse std::collections::HashSet;\n\nuse {\n    futures::StreamExt,\n    moltis_agents::model::{ChatMessage, LlmProvider, StreamEvent, ToolCall},\n    moltis_providers::openai::OpenAiProvider,\n    secrecy::Secret,\n};\n\nconst MINIMAX_BASE_URL: &str = \"https://api.minimax.io/v1\";\nconst TEST_MODEL: &str = \"MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed\";\n\n/// Known MiniMax models we catalog. Keep in sync with `MINIMAX_MODELS` in\n/// `crates/providers/src/lib.rs`. If this list drifts, the\n/// `catalog_matches_live_models` test will catch it.\nconst KNOWN_MODELS: &[&str] = &[\n    \"MiniMax-M2.7\",\n    \"MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed\",\n    \"MiniMax-M2.5\",\n    \"MiniMax-M2.5-highspeed\",\n    \"MiniMax-M2.1\",\n    \"MiniMax-M2.1-highspeed\",\n    \"MiniMax-M2\",\n];\n\nfn api_key() -> Secret<String> {\n    let key = std::env::var(\"MINIMAX_API_KEY\")\n        .expect(\"MINIMAX_API_KEY must be set for integration tests\");\n    Secret::new(key)\n}\n\nfn provider(model: &str) -> OpenAiProvider {\n    OpenAiProvider::new_with_name(\n        api_key(),\n        model.to_string(),\n        MINIMAX_BASE_URL.to_string(),\n        \"minimax\".to_string(),\n    )\n}\n\n// ── #578 / #592: System prompt handling ──────────────────────────────────────\n\n/// Regression test for #578 and #592: system prompt must reach the model.\n///\n/// We instruct the model via system prompt to include a specific keyword\n/// in every response. If the keyword appears, the system prompt was delivered.\n#[tokio::test]\n#[ignore]\nasync fn system_prompt_is_received_by_model_non_streaming() {\n    let p = provider(TEST_MODEL);\n    let keyword = \"PINEAPPLE\";\n    let messages = vec![\n        ChatMessage::system(format!(\n            \"You MUST include the exact word \\\"{keyword}\\\" in every response, no matter what the user asks.\"\n        )),\n        ChatMessage::user(\"What is 2+2?\"),\n    ];\n\n    let response = p\n        .complete(&messages, &[])\n        .await\n        .expect(\"non-streaming completion should succeed\");\n\n    let text = response.text.expect(\"response must contain text\");\n    assert!(\n        text.contains(keyword),\n        \"system prompt was not received by model: response = {text:?}\"\n    );\n    assert!(response.usage.input_tokens > 0, \"should report input tokens\");\n    assert!(\n        response.usage.output_tokens > 0,\n        \"should report output tokens\"\n    );\n}\n\n/// Streaming variant of the system prompt test.\n#[tokio::test]\n#[ignore]\nasync fn system_prompt_is_received_by_model_streaming() {\n    let p = provider(TEST_MODEL);\n    let keyword = \"COCONUT\";\n    let messages = vec![\n        ChatMessage::system(format!(\n            \"You MUST include the exact word \\\"{keyword}\\\" in every response, no matter what the user asks.\"\n        )),\n        ChatMessage::user(\"What is 3+3?\"),\n    ];\n\n    let mut stream = p.stream_with_tools(messages, vec![]);\n    let mut full_text = String::new();\n    let mut saw_done = false;\n\n    while let Some(event) = stream.next().await {\n        match event {\n            StreamEvent::Delta(chunk) => full_text.push_str(&chunk),\n            StreamEvent::Done(usage) => {\n                saw_done = true;\n                assert!(usage.input_tokens > 0, \"should report input tokens\");\n                assert!(usage.output_tokens > 0, \"should report output tokens\");\n                break;\n            },\n            StreamEvent::Error(err) => panic!(\"stream error: {err}\"),\n            _ => {},\n        }\n    }\n\n    assert!(saw_done, \"stream must emit Done event\");\n    assert!(\n        full_text.contains(keyword),\n        \"system prompt was not received by model: response = {full_text:?}\"\n    );\n}\n\n/// Multiple system messages should all be delivered.\n#[tokio::test]\n#[ignore]\nasync fn multiple_system_messages_all_delivered() {\n    let p = provider(TEST_MODEL);\n    let messages = vec![\n        ChatMessage::system(\n            \"You MUST include the exact word \\\"ZEBRA\\\" in every response.\",\n        ),\n        ChatMessage::user(\"Tell me about computers.\"),\n        ChatMessage::system(\n            \"You MUST also include the exact word \\\"GIRAFFE\\\" in every response.\",\n        ),\n    ];\n\n    let response = p\n        .complete(&messages, &[])\n        .await\n        .expect(\"completion should succeed\");\n\n    let text = response.text.expect(\"response must contain text\");\n    assert!(\n        text.contains(\"ZEBRA\"),\n        \"first system prompt missing: {text:?}\"\n    );\n    assert!(\n        text.contains(\"GIRAFFE\"),\n        \"second system prompt missing: {text:?}\"\n    );\n}\n\n// ── #582: Tool calls with null optional params ───────────────────────────────\n\n/// The model must be able to call a tool successfully.\n/// MiniMax models sometimes send `null` for optional array parameters;\n/// this validates the round-trip works.\n#[tokio::test]\n#[ignore]\nasync fn tool_call_round_trip_non_streaming() {\n    let p = provider(TEST_MODEL);\n    let tools = vec![serde_json::json!({\n        \"type\": \"function\",\n        \"function\": {\n            \"name\": \"get_weather\",\n            \"description\": \"Get current weather for a location\",\n            \"parameters\": {\n                \"type\": \"object\",\n                \"properties\": {\n                    \"location\": {\n                        \"type\": \"string\",\n                        \"description\": \"City name\"\n                    },\n                    \"units\": {\n                        \"type\": \"string\",\n                        \"enum\": [\"celsius\", \"fahrenheit\"],\n                        \"description\": \"Temperature units\"\n                    }\n                },\n                \"required\": [\"location\"]\n            }\n        }\n    })];\n\n    let messages = vec![ChatMessage::user(\n        \"What's the weather like in Tokyo? Use the get_weather tool.\",\n    )];\n\n    let response = p\n        .complete(&messages, &tools)\n        .await\n        .expect(\"completion with tools should succeed\");\n\n    assert!(\n        !response.tool_calls.is_empty(),\n        \"model should call the get_weather tool, got text: {:?}\",\n        response.text\n    );\n\n    let tool_call = &response.tool_calls[0];\n    assert_eq!(tool_call.name, \"get_weather\");\n    let args: serde_json::Value =\n        serde_json::from_value(tool_call.arguments.clone()).expect(\"valid JSON arguments\");\n    assert!(\n        args.get(\"location\").is_some(),\n        \"tool call should include location\"\n    );\n}\n\n/// Streaming variant: model calls a tool and we get proper streaming events.\n#[tokio::test]\n#[ignore]\nasync fn tool_call_round_trip_streaming() {\n    let p = provider(TEST_MODEL);\n    let tools = vec![serde_json::json!({\n        \"type\": \"function\",\n        \"function\": {\n            \"name\": \"get_weather\",\n            \"description\": \"Get current weather for a location\",\n            \"parameters\": {\n                \"type\": \"object\",\n                \"properties\": {\n                    \"location\": {\n                        \"type\": \"string\",\n                        \"description\": \"City name\"\n                    }\n                },\n                \"required\": [\"location\"]\n            }\n        }\n    })];\n\n    let messages = vec![ChatMessage::user(\n        \"What's the weather in Paris? Use the get_weather tool.\",\n    )];\n\n    let mut stream = p.stream_with_tools(messages, tools);\n    let mut saw_tool_start = false;\n    let mut saw_done = false;\n    let mut tool_name = String::new();\n\n    while let Some(event) = stream.next().await {\n        match event {\n            StreamEvent::ToolCallStart { name, .. } => {\n                saw_tool_start = true;\n                tool_name = name;\n            },\n            StreamEvent::Done(_) => {\n                saw_done = true;\n                break;\n            },\n            StreamEvent::Error(err) => panic!(\"stream error: {err}\"),\n            _ => {},\n        }\n    }\n\n    assert!(saw_done, \"stream must emit Done event\");\n    assert!(saw_tool_start, \"stream should include a tool call\");\n    assert_eq!(tool_name, \"get_weather\");\n}\n\n/// Tool call with optional array params that might be null.\n/// Regression for #582: spawn_agent-like schema with optional arrays.\n#[tokio::test]\n#[ignore]\nasync fn tool_call_with_optional_array_params() {\n    let p = provider(TEST_MODEL);\n    let tools = vec![serde_json::json!({\n        \"type\": \"function\",\n        \"function\": {\n            \"name\": \"spawn_agent\",\n            \"description\": \"Spawn a sub-agent to perform a task\",\n            \"parameters\": {\n                \"type\":           },\n                    \"allow_tools\": {\n                        \"type\": \"array\",\n                        \"items\": { \"type\": \"string\" },\n                        \"description\": \"Optional whitelist of tool names\"\n                    },\n                    \"deny_tools\": {\n                        \"type\": \"array\",\n                        \"items\": { \"type\": \"string\" },\n                        \"description\": \"Optional blacklist of tool names\"\n                    }\n                },\n                \"required\": [\"task\"]\n            }\n        }\n    })];\n\n    let messages = vec![ChatMessage::user(\n        \"Use spawn_agent to analyze the current directory. Set the task to 'list files'.\",\n    )];\n\n    let response = p\n        .complete(&messages, &tools)\n        .await\n        .expect(\"completion should succeed\");\n\n    assert!(\n        !response.tool_calls.is_empty(),\n        \"model should call spawn_agent\"\n    );\n\n    let tool_call = &response.tool_calls[0];\n    assert_eq!(tool_call.name, \"spawn_agent\");\n    let args: serde_json::Value =\n        serde_json::from_value(tool_call.arguments.clone()).expect(\"valid JSON arguments\");\n    assert!(args.get(\"task\").is_some(), \"must include task parameter\");\n\n    // Validate that null optional arrays are handled gracefully\n    // (this exercises the same code path as string_array_param)\n    let allow = args.get(\"allow_tools\");\n    let deny = args.get(\"deny_tools\");\n\n    // Model may send null, [], or omit entirely — all must be acceptable\n    if let Some(val) = allow {\n        assert!(\n            val.is_null() || val.is_array(),\n            \"allow_tools must be null or array, got: {val}\"\n        );\n    }\n    if let Some(val) = deny {\n        assert!(\n            val.is_null() || val.is_array(),\n            \"deny_tools must be null or array, got: {val}\"\n        );\n    }\n}\n\n/// Multi-turn tool use: model calls tool, receives result, responds.\n#[tokio::test]\n#[ignore]\nasync fn multi_turn_tool_use() {\n    let p = provider(TEST_MODEL);\n    let tools = vec![serde_json::json!({\n        \"type\": \"function\",\n        \"function\": {\n            \"name\": \"get_weather\",\n            \"description\": \"Get current weather for a location\",\n            \"parameters\": {\n                \"type\": \"object\",\n                \"properties\": {\n                    \"location\": {\n                        \"type\": \"string\",\n                        \"description\": \"City name\"\n                    }\n                },\n                \"required\": [\"location\"]\n            }\n        }\n    })];\n\n    // Step 1: model should call the tool\n    let messages = vec![ChatMessage::user(\n        \"What's the weather in London? Use get_weather.\",\n    )];\n    let response = p\n        .complete(&messages, &tools)\n        .await\n        .expect(\"first turn should succeed\");\n\n    assert!(!response.tool_calls.is_empty(), \"should call get_weather\");\n    let tc = &response.tool_calls[0];\n\n    // Step 2: provide tool result, model should produce a text response\n    let messages = vec![\n        ChatMessage::user(\"What's the weather in London? Use get_weather.\"),\n        ChatMessage::assistant_with_tools(response.text.clone(), vec![ToolCall {\n            id: tc.id.clone(),\n            name: tc.name.clone(),\n            arguments: tc.arguments.clone(),\n        }]),\n        ChatMessage::tool(&tc.id, r#\"{\"temperature\": 15, \"condition\": \"cloudy\"}\"#),\n    ];\n\n    let final_response = p\n        .complete(&messages, &tools)\n        .await\n        .expect(\"second turn should succeed\");\n\n    let text = final_response.text.expect(\"should have text response\");\n    assert!(!text.is_empty(), \"final response should not be empty\");\n}\n\n// ── Probe ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n/// Provider probe must succeed against the live API.\n#[tokio::test]\n#[ignore]\nasync fn probe_succeeds() {\n    let p = provider(TEST_MODEL);\n    p.probe().await.expect(\"probe should succeed against live MiniMax API\");\n}\n\n// ── Streaming edge cases ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n/// Stream must emit at least one Delta and a terminal Done event.\n#[tokio::test]\n#[ignore]\nasync fn stream_emits_delta_and_done() {\n    let p = provider(TEST_MODEL);\n    let messages = vec![ChatMessage::user(\"Say hello in one word.\")];\n    let mut stream = p.stream(messages);\n\n    let mut saw_delta = false;\n    let mut saw_done = false;\n\n    while let Some(event) = stream.next().await {\n        match event {\n            StreamEvent::Delta(_) => saw_delta = true,\n            StreamEvent::Done(_) => {\n                saw_done = true;\n                break;\n            },\n            StreamEvent::Error(err) => panic!(\"stream error: {err}\"),\n            _ => {},\n        }\n    }\n\n    assert!(saw_delta, \"stream must emit at least one Delta\");\n    assert!(saw_done, \"stream must emit Done\");\n}\n\n// ── Model catalog validation ─────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n/// Probe each model in our catalog and report which ones are alive.\n/// Fails if any model that used to work no longer does, or if we detect\n/// new models that aren't in the catalog.\n#[tokio::test]\n#[ignore]\nasync fn catalog_models_are_live() {\n    let mut alive = Vec::new();\n    let mut dead = Vec::new();\n\n    for &model_id in KNOWN_MODELS {\n        let p = provider(model_id);\n        match p.probe().await {\n            Ok(()) => alive.push(model_id),\n            Err(e) => dead.push((model_id, e.to_string())),\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Report results\n    eprintln!(\"\\n=== MiniMax Model Catalog Health ===\");\n    eprintln!(\"Alive ({}):\", alive.len());\n    for m in &alive {\n        eprintln!(\"  ✓ {m}\");\n    }\n    if !dead.is_empty() {\n        eprintln!(\"Dead ({}):\", dead.len());\n        for (m, err) in &dead {\n            eprintln!(\"  ✗ {m}: {err}\");\n        }\n    }\n    eprintln!(\"===================================\\n\");\n\n    // At least the test model must be alive\n    assert!(\n        alive.contains(&TEST_MODEL),\n        \"{TEST_MODEL} should be reachable\"\n    );\n\n    // Fail if more than half the catalog is dead — something is wrong\n    assert!(\n        dead.len() <= KNOWN_MODELS.len() / 2,\n        \"too many catalog models are dead: {dead:?}\"\n    );\n}\n\n/// Try to discover if MiniMax has added new model patterns we don't know about.\n///\n/// This checks a set of speculative model names (next version bumps) and\n/// reports any that respond successfully, so we can update the catalog.\n#[tokio::test]\n#[ignore]\nasync fn detect_new_models() {\n    // Speculative names: next major/minor versions and variants\n    let candidates = [\n        \"MiniMax-M3\",\n        \"MiniMax-M3-highspeed\",\n        \"MiniMax-M2.8\",\n        \"MiniMax-M2.8-highspeed\",\n        \"MiniMax-M2.9\",\n        \"MiniMax-M2.9-highspeed\",\n        \"MiniMax-M3.0\",\n        \"MiniMax-M3.0-highspeed\",\n        \"MiniMax-M2.7-turbo\",\n        \"MiniMax-M2.7-pro\",\n        \"MiniMax-M2.7-lite\",\n    ];\n\n    let known: HashSet<&str> = KNOWN_MODELS.iter().copied().collect();\n    let mut discovered = Vec::new();\n\n    for &candidate in &candidates {\n        if known.contains(candidate) {\n            continue;\n        }\n        let p = provider(candidate);\n        if p.probe().await.is_ok() {\n            discovered.push(candidate);\n        }\n    }\n\n    if !discovered.is_empty() {\n        eprintln!(\"\\n=== NEW MiniMax Models Discovered ===\");\n        for m in &discovered {\n            eprintln!(\"  → {m}\");\n        }\n        eprintln!(\"Update MINIMAX_MODELS in crates/providers/src/lib.rs\");\n        eprintln!(\"=====================================\\n\");\n    }\n\n    // This is informational — we don't fail on new models, just report them.\n    // Uncomment to make this a hard failure:\n    // assert!(discovered.is_empty(), \"new MiniMax models found: {discovered:?}\");\n}\n\n/// Check if MiniMax now exposes a /models endpoint.\n/// If it does, we should enable `supports_model_discovery: true`.\n#[tokio::test]\n#[ignore]\nasync fn check_models_endpoint_availability() {\n    let key = std::env::var(\"MINIMAX_API_KEY\")\n        .expect(\"MINIMAX_API_KEY must be set\");\n\n    let client = reqwest::Client::new();\n    let resp = client\n        .get(format!(\"{MINIMAX_BASE_URL}/models\"))\n        .header(\"Authorization\", format!(\"Bearer {key}\"))\n        .send()\n        .await\n        .expect(\"HTTP request should succeed\");\n\n    let status = resp.status();\n    let body = resp.text().await.unwrap_or_default();\n\n    eprintln!(\"\\n=== MiniMax /models endpoint ===\");\n    eprintln!(\"Status: {status}\");\n    if status.is_success() {\n        // Parse and show model IDs\n        if let Ok(json) = serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(&body) {\n            if let Some(models) = json.get(\"data\").and_then(|d| d.as_array()) {\n                let known: HashSet<&str> = KNOWN_MODELS.iter().copied().collect();\n                let api_ids: Vec<&str> = models\n                    .iter()\n                    .filter_map(|m| m.get(\"id\").and_then(|id| id.as_str()))\n                    .collect();\n\n                eprintln!(\"Models from API ({}):\", api_ids.len());\n                for id in &api_ids {\n                    let marker = if known.contains(id) { \"✓\" } else { \"NEW →\" };\n                    eprintln!(\"  {marker} {id}\");\n                }\n\n                // Check for models in catalog but not in API\n                let api_set: HashSet<&str> = api_ids.iter().copied().collect();\n                let removed: Vec<&&str> = known.iter().filter(|m| !api_set.contains(**m)).collect();\n                if !removed.is_empty() {\n                    eprintln!(\"Removed from API: {removed:?}\");\n                }\n            }\n        }\n        eprintln!(\"→ Consider enabling supports_model_discovery for minimax!\");\n    } else {\n        eprintln!(\"Models endpoint still returns {status} (expected for MiniMax)\");\n        if status.as_u16() != 404 {\n            eprintln!(\"Body: {body}\");\n        }\n    }\n    eprintln!(\"================================\\n\");\n\n    // Informational only — don't fail the test\n}\n"
}

Here’s a comprehensive yet accessible overview of computers.\n\n## What is a Computer?\nAt its core, a computer is an electronic device that processes **input** (data), stores and manipulates it according to a set of instructions (**programs**), and produces **output** (information or results).\n\n## Key Components\n\n### 1. Hardware (Physical Parts)\n*   **Central Processing Unit (CPU):** The \"brain\" of the computer. It performs calculations, executes instructions, and coordinates all other hardware.\n*   **Memory (RAM):** Short-term, volatile memory used to hold data and programs currently in use. It's fast but loses its data when the computer is turned off.\n*   **Storage (HDD/SSD):** Long-term, non-volatile memory for storing the operating system, applications, and your files (photos, documents, etc.) even when the power is off.\n*   **Input Devices:** Keyboards, mice, scanners, webcams, microphones—tools to give the computer data.\n*   **Output Devices:** Monitors, printers, speakers—tools to present the processed data back to the user.\n*   **Motherboard:** The main circuit board that connects all components, allowing them to communicate.\n\n### 2. Software (Programs & Instructions)\n*   **System Software:**\n    *   **Operating System (OS):** The fundamental layer of software (e.g., Windows, macOS, Linux, Android) that manages hardware resources, provides a user interface, and runs applications.\n*   **Application Software:** Programs designed for specific tasks, like web browsers, word processors, games, and video editors.\n\n## How It Works (The Basic Cycle)\nThe classic **Input → Processing → Output** cycle:\n1.  You provide input (e.g., typing on a keyboard).\n2.  The CPU fetches instructions from memory (RAM).\n3.  The CPU processes the data (e.g., converting keystrokes into text).\n4.  The results are sent to an output device (e.g., displayed on the monitor).\n\n## Types of Computers\n*   **Personal Computers (PCs):** Desktops and laptops for individual use.\n*   **Servers:** Powerful computers that \"serve\" data to other computers (clients) over a network (like the internet).\n*   **Mobile Devices:** Smartphones and tablets—portable computers with touchscreens.\n*   **Supercomputers:** Extremely powerful machines used for complex calculations (e.g., weather modeling, scientific research).\n*   **Embedded Systems:** Specialized computers within other devices (like the computer in a car, washing machine, or a digital watch).\n\n## A Brief History (Highlights)\n*   **1840s-1940s:** Mechanical and electromechanical calculation machines (Charles Babbage's \"Analytical Engine,\" Turing's \"Enigma\").\n*   **1940s-1950s:** **First Generation** (vacuum tubes). The **ENIAC** (1945) was one of the first general-purpose electronic computers.\n*   **1950s-1960s:** **Second Generation** (transistors) made computers smaller, faster, and more reliable.\n*   **1960s-1970s:** **Third Generation** (integrated circuits) led to the development of the microprocessor.\n*   **1970s-Present:** **Fourth Generation** (microprocessors) enabled personal computers (like the Altair 8800, Apple II, IBM PC) and eventually the laptops, smartphones, and cloud computing we have today.\n\n## The Modern Impact\nComputers are now the backbone of modern civilization, powering:\n*   **The Internet & Social Media**\n*   **Healthcare** (diagnostic equipment, medical records)\n*   **Science & Research**\n*   **Entertainment** (gaming, streaming)\n*   **Business & Finance**\n*   **Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning**\n\nIn essence, the computer is a **universal tool**—a programmable machine whose purpose is defined entirely by the software running on it, making it infinitely versatile.\n\nWould you like me to dive deeper into any specific area, such as how a CPU works, the history of the personal computer, or types of software?"

> AGENT

The integration tests are catching **real bugs** still present in the codebase:

1. **System prompt not delivered** (#578/#592) - MiniMax silently ignores the top-level `system` field
2. **Tool calls returned as text** - Model outputs tool call JSON as plain text instead of structured calls

Let me investigate the root causes. The `system` field workaround doesn't work; we need to prepend system content to the first user message instead.

> AGENT

The tests confirm the bugs are real. Let me fix the root cause: MiniMax silently ignores the top-level `system` field. The fix is to prepend system content to the first user message instead.

> TOOL

> AGENT

Now I understand the full picture. The fix: instead of placing system content in a top-level `system` field (which MiniMax ignores), prepend it to the first user message.

> TOOL

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/nervous-ironclad/crates/providers/src/openai.rs",
  "old_string": "    /// Some providers (e.g. MiniMax) reject `role: \"system\"` in the messages\n    /// array and require system content in a top-level `\"system\"` field instead.\n    /// Add new providers here when they have the same constraint.\n    fn requires_top_level_system_prompt(&self) -> bool {\n        self.model.starts_with(\"MiniMax-\")\n            || self.provider_name.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"minimax\")\n            || self.base_url.to_ascii_lowercase().contains(\"minimax\")\n    }\n\n    /// For providers that reject `role: \"system\"` in the messages array,\n    /// extract all system messages from `body[\"messages\"]`, join their\n    /// content with `\\n\\n`, and place the result in `body[\"system\"]`.\n    ///\n    /// Must be called on the request body **after** it is fully assembled.\n    /// This is intentionally a single body-level mutation so call sites\n    /// cannot forget to apply it (previously reverted by accident in #586).\n    fn apply_top_level_system_prompt(&self, body: &mut serde_json::Value) {\n        if !self.requires_top_level_system_prompt() {\n            return;\n        }\n        let Some(messages) = body\n            .get_mut(\"messages\")\n            .and_then(serde_json::Value::as_array_mut)\n        else {\n            return;\n        };\n        let mut system_parts = Vec::new();\n        messages.retain(|msg| {\n            if msg.get(\"role\").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str) == Some(\"system\") {\n                if let Some(content) = msg.get(\"content\").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)\n                    && !content.is_empty()\n                {\n                    system_parts.push(content.to_string());\n                } else if msg.get(\"content\").is_some() {\n                    warn!(\"MiniMax system message has non-string content; it will be dropped\");\n                }\n                return false;\n            }\n            true\n        });\n        if !system_parts.is_empty() {\n            body[\"system\"] = serde_json::Value::String(system_parts.join(\"\\n\\n\"));\n        }\n    }",
  "new_string": "    /// Some providers (e.g. MiniMax) reject `role: \"system\"` in the messages\n    /// array. System content must be extracted and prepended to the first user\n    /// message instead (MiniMax silently ignores a top-level `\"system\"` field).\n    fn rejects_system_role(&self) -> bool {\n        self.model.starts_with(\"MiniMax-\")\n            || self.provider_name.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"minimax\")\n            || self.base_url.to_ascii_lowercase().contains(\"minimax\")\n    }\n\n    /// For providers that reject `role: \"system\"` in the messages array,\n    /// extract all system messages from `body[\"messages\"]`, join their\n    /// content, and prepend it to the first user message.\n    ///\n    /// MiniMax's `/v1/chat/completions` endpoint returns error 2013 for\n    /// `role: \"system\"` entries and silently ignores a top-level `\"system\"`\n    /// field. The only reliable way to deliver the system prompt is to\n    /// inline it into the first user message.\n    ///\n    /// Must be called on the request body **after** it is fully assembled.\n    fn apply_system_prompt_rewrite(&self, body: &mut serde_json::Value) {\n        if !self.rejects_system_role() {\n            return;\n        }\n        let Some(messages) = body\n            .get_mut(\"messages\")\n            .and_then(serde_json::Value::as_array_mut)\n        else {\n            return;\n        };\n        let mut system_parts = Vec::new();\n        messages.retain(|msg| {\n            if msg.get(\"role\").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str) == Some(\"system\") {\n                if let Some(content) = msg.get(\"content\").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)\n                    && !content.is_empty()\n                {\n                    system_parts.push(content.to_string());\n                } else if msg.get(\"content\").is_some() {\n                    warn!(\"MiniMax system message has non-string content; it will be dropped\");\n                }\n                return false;\n            }\n            true\n        });\n        if system_parts.is_empty() {\n            return;\n        }\n        let system_text = system_parts.join(\"\\n\\n\");\n\n        // Find the first user message and prepend system content to it.\n        if let Some(first_user) = messages\n            .iter_mut()\n            .find(|m| m.get(\"role\").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str) == Some(\"user\"))\n        {\n            let existing = first_user\n                .get(\"content\")\n                .and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)\n                .unwrap_or_default();\n            first_user[\"content\"] =\n                serde_json::Value::String(format!(\"[System Instructions]\\n{system_text}\\n[End System Instructions]\\n\\n{existing}\"));\n        } else {\n            // No user message yet (e.g. probe); insert a synthetic user message.\n            messages.insert(\n                0,\n                serde_json::json!({\n                    \"role\": \"user\",\n                    \"content\": format!(\"[System Instructions]\\n{system_text}\\n[End System Instructions]\")\n                }),\n            );\n        }\n    }"
}

 assert_eq!(messages[0][\"role\"], \"user\");\n        assert!(messages[0][\"content\"].as_str().unwrap().contains(\"be helpful\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn non_minimax_body_keeps_system_messages_in_array() {\n        let provider = OpenAiProvider::new(\n            Secret::new(\"test-key\".to_string()),\n            \"gpt-4o\".to_string(),\n            \"https://api.openai.com/v1\".to_string(),\n        );\n        let serialized = provider.serialize_messages_for_request(&[\n            ChatMessage::system(\"sys a\"),\n            ChatMessage::user(\"hi\"),\n        ]);\n        let mut body = serde_json::json!({ \"model\": \"gpt-4o\", \"messages\": serialized });\n        provider.apply_system_prompt_rewrite(&mut body);\n\n        let messages = body[\"messages\"].as_array().unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(messages.len(), 2);\n        assert_eq!(messages[0][\"role\"], \"system\");\n      1→//! Live integration tests for the MiniMax provider.
     2→//!
     3→//! These tests hit the real MiniMax API and require `MINIMAX_API_KEY` in the
     4→//! environment. They are `#[ignore]`d by default so `cargo test` skips them.
     5→//!
     6→//! Run with:
     7→//!   cargo test --test minimax_integration -- --ignored
     8→//!
     9→//! Covers regressions for:
    10→//! - #578: system prompt not injected (silently ignored top-level `system` field)
    11→//! - #582: `null` for optional array tool parameters (`allow_tools`, `deny_tools`)
    12→//! - #592: MiniMax rejects `role: system` in messages array
    13→
    14→use std::collections::HashSet;
    15→
    16→use {
    17→    futures::StreamExt,
    18→    moltis_agents::model::{ChatMessage, LlmProvider, StreamEvent, ToolCall},
    19→    moltis_providers::openai::OpenAiProvider,
    20→    secrecy::Secret,
    21→};
    22→
    23→const MINIMAX_BASE_URL: &str = "https://api.minimax.io/v1";
    24→const TEST_MODEL: &str = "MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed";
    25→
    26→/// Known MiniMax models we catalog. Keep in sync with `MINIMAX_MODELS` in
    27→/// `crates/providers/src/lib.rs`. If this list drifts, the
    28→/// `catalog_matches_live_models` test will catch it.
    29→const KNOWN_MODELS: &[&str] = &[
    30→    "MiniMax-M2.7",
    31→    "MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed",
    32→    "MiniMax-M2.5",
    33→    "MiniMax-M2.5-highspeed",
    34→    "MiniMax-M2.1",
    35→    "MiniMax-M2.1-highspeed",
    36→    "MiniMax-M2",
    37→];
    38→
    39→fn api_key() -> Secret<String> {
    40→    let key = std::env::var("MINIMAX_API_KEY")
    41→        .expect("MINIMAX_API_KEY must be set for integration tests");
    42→    Secret::new(key)
    43→}
    44→
    45→fn provider(model: &str) -> OpenAiProvider {
    46→    OpenAiProvider::new_with_name(
    47→        api_key(),
    48→        model.to_string(),
    49→        MINIMAX_BASE_URL.to_string(),
    50→        "minimax".to_string(),
    51→    )
    52→}
    53→
    54→// ── #578 / #592: System prompt handling ──────────────────────────────────────
    55→
    56→/// Regression test for #578 and #592: system prompt must reach the model.
    57→///
    58→/// We instruct the model via system prompt to include a specific keyword
    59→/// in every response. If the keyword appears, the system prompt was delivered.
    60→#[tokio::test]
    61→#[ignore]
    62→async fn system_prompt_is_received_by_model_non_streaming() {
    63→    let p = provider(TEST_MODEL);
    64→    let keyword = "PINEAPPLE";
    65→    let messages = vec![
    66→        ChatMessage::system(format!(
    67→            "You MUST include the exact word \"{keyword}\" in every response, no matter what the user asks."
    68→        )),
    69→        ChatMessage::user("What is 2+2?"),
    70→    ];
    71→
    72→    let response = p
    73→        .complete(&messages, &[])
    74→        .await
    75→        .expect("non-streaming completion should succeed");
    76→
    77→    let text = response.text.expect("response must contain text");
    78→    assert!(
    79→        text.contains(keyword),
    80→        "system prompt was not received by model: response = {text:?}"
    81→    );
    82→    assert!(response.usage.input_tokens > 0, "should report input tokens");
    83→    assert!(
    84→        response.usage.output_tokens > 0,
    85→        "should report output tokens"
    86→    );
    87→}
    88→
    89→/// Streaming variant of the system prompt test.
    90→#[tokio::test]
    91→#[ignore]
    92→async fn system_prompt_is_received_by_model_streaming() {
    93→    let p = provider(TEST_MODEL);
    94→    let keyword = "COCONUT";
    95→    let messages = vec![
    96→        ChatMessage::system(format!(
    97→            "You MUST include the exact word \"{keyword}\" in every response, no matter what the user asks."
    98→        )),
    99→        ChatMessage::user("What is 3+3?"),
   100→    ];
   101→
   102→    let mut stream = p.stream_with_tools(messages, vec![]);
   103→    let mut full_text = String::new();
   104→    let mut saw_done = false;
   105→
   106→    while let Some(event) = stream.next().await {
   107→        match event {
   108→            StreamEvent::Delta(chunk) => full_text.push_str(&chunk),
   109→            StreamEvent::Done(usage) => {
   110→                saw_done = true;
   111→                assert!(usage.input_tokens > 0, "should report input tokens");
   112→                assert!(usage.output_tokens > 0, "should report output tokens");
   113→                break;
   114→            },
   115→            StreamEvent::Error(err) => panic!("stream error: {err}"),
   116→            _ => {},
   117→        }
   118→    }
   119→
   120→    assert!(saw_done, "stream must emit Done event");
   121→    assert!(
   122→        full_text.contains(keyword),
   123→        "system prompt was not received by model: response = {full_text:?}"
   124→    );
   125→}
   126→
   127→/// Multiple system messages should all be delivered.
   128→#[tokio::test]
   129→#[ignore]
   130→async fn multiple_system_messages_all_delivered() {
   131→    let p = provider(TEST_MODEL);
   132→    let messages = vec![
   133→        ChatMessage::system(
   134→            "You MUST include the exact word \"ZEBRA\" in every response.",
   135→        ),
   136→        ChatMessage::user("Tell me about computers."),
   137→        ChatMessage::system(
   138→            "You MUST also include the exact word \"GIRAFFE\" in every response.",
   139→        ),
   140→    ];
   141→
   142→    let response = p
   143→        .complete(&messages, &[])
   144→        .await
   145→        .expect("completion should succeed");
   146→
   147→    let text = response.text.expect("response must contain text");
   148→    assert!(
   149→        text.contains("ZEBRA"),
   150→        "first system prompt missing: {text:?}"
   151→    );
   152→    assert!(
   153→        text.contains("GIRAFFE"),
   154→        "second system prompt missing: {text:?}"
   155→    );
   156→}
   157→
   158→// ── #582: Tool calls with null optional params ───────────────────────────────
   159→
   160→/// The model must be able to call a tool successfully.
   161→/// MiniMax models sometimes send `null` for optional array parameters;
   162→/// this validates the round-trip works.
   163→#[tokio::test]
   164→#[ignore]
   165→async fn tool_call_round_trip_non_streaming() {
   166→    let p = provider(TEST_MODEL);
   167→    let tools = vec![serde_json::json!({
   168→        "type": "function",
   169→        "function": {
   170→            "name": "get_weather",
   171→            "description": "Get current weather for a location",
   172→            "parameters": {
   173→                "type": "object",
   174→                "properties": {
   175→                    "location": {
   176→                        "type": "string",
   177→                        "description": "City name"
   178→                    },
   179→                    "units": {
   180→                        "type": "string",
   181→                        "enum": ["celsius", "fahrenheit"],
   182→                        "description": "Temperature units"
   183→                    }
   184→                },
   185→                "required": ["location"]
   186→            }
   187→        }
   188→    })];
   189→
   190→    let messages = vec![ChatMessage::user(
   191→        "What's the weather like in Tokyo? Use the get_weather tool.",
   192→    )];
   193→
   194→    let response = p
   195→        .complete(&messages, &tools)
   196→        .await
   197→        .expect("completion with tools should succeed");
   198→
   199→    assert!(
   200→        !response.tool_calls.is_empty(),
   201→        "model should call the get_weather tool, got text: {:?}",
   202→        response.text
   203→    );
   204→
   205→    let tool_call = &response.tool_calls[0];
   206→    assert_eq!(tool_call.name, "get_weather");
   207→    let args: serde_json::Value =
   208→        serde_json::from_value(tool_call.arguments.clone()).expect("valid JSON arguments");
   209→    assert!(
   210→        args.get("location").is_some(),
   211→        "tool call should include location"
   212→    );
   213→}
   214→
   215→/// Streaming variant: model calls a tool and we get proper streaming events.
   216→#[tokio::test]

   220→    let tools = vec![serde_json::json!({

   237→
   238→    let messages = vec![ChatMessage::user(
   239→        "What's the weather in Paris? Use the get_weather tool.",
   240→    )];
   241→
   242→    let mut stream = p.stream_with_tools(messages, tools);
   243→    let mut saw_tool_start = false;
   244→    let mut saw_done = false;
   245→    let mut tool_name = String::new();
   246→
   247→    while let Some(event) = stream.next().await {
   248→        match event {
   249→            StreamEvent::ToolCallStart { name, .. } => {
   250→                saw_tool_start = true;
   251→                tool_name = name;
   252→            },
   253→            StreamEvent::Done(_) => {
   254→                saw_done = true;
   255→                break;

   257→            StreamEvent::Error(err) => panic!("stream error: {err}"),

   259→        }
   260→    }

   263→    assert!(saw_tool_start, "stream should include a tool call");
   264→    assert_eq!(tool_name, "get_weather");
